Patrick Dempsey's Thanksgiving Horror Movie Is Worth Revisiting This Holiday Season

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Eli Roth's 2023 slasher flick "Thanksgiving" follows various citizens of Plymouth, Massachusetts, as they find themselves being targeted by a killer dressed as the famed Pilgrim John Carver in the lead-up to the titular holiday. Realizing that these murders are related to a Black Friday trampling incident from the previous year (which led to three people dying), it falls to Plymouth's sheriff, Eric Newlon (Patrick Dempsey), to figure out who's been taking some Turkey Day-related revenge.

There aren't too many scary flicks centered around this particular holiday, but Roth's film has now joined the modest pantheon of Thanksgiving horror movies that includes Brad F. Grinter's schlock classic "Blood Freak," the utterly absurd "ThanksKilling," the pointed anticapitalist flick "Black Friday," and Oliver Blackburn's "Kristy." Indeed, Roth's "Thanksgiving" is the perfect horror movie to watch this November, or any November, really. Like most of his other R-rated offerings, its gore is gleefully over the top, and there's gallows humor present throughout.

"Thanksgiving" began its life as a fake trailer that Roth directed for 2007's two-in-one "Grindhouse," itself an attempt by directors Robert Rodriguez and Quentin Tarantino to recreate the experience of going to a rundown grindhouse double feature circa the 1970s or '80s. They each helmed one down-and-dirty B-movie, giving them fake film scratches and print wear to boot. The program also featured a brief reel of previews for non-existent B-movies, one of which was the gory slasher "Thanksgiving." It took Roth 16 years, but he later expanded that fake promo into a real feature film. Now, it's appointment holiday viewing.

"Thanksgiving" was, by dint of its novelty, something of a hit. On a budget of only $15 million, it made $46.6 million at the box office and, perhaps surprisingly, earned pretty good reviews. Not only that, "Thanksgiving" also has a higher critical approval score on Rotten Tomatoes than any of Eli Roth's other feature-length directorial efforts, among them the mostly well-received "The House with a Clock in Its Walls" and the critically lambasted bomb "Borderlands."

"Thanksgiving" seemingly got a lot of mileage from its creative kills. One victim, for instance, is murdered, cooked, trussed up like a turkey, and served at a table surrounded by hostages and victims. (This kill is featured in Roth's original fake trailer as well.) In addition, "Thanksgiving" features the kind of fun, splattery deaths that slasher fans crave, among them a decapitation, a disemboweling, and a trampoline stabbing.

Meanwhile, Patrick Dempsey's character is crucial to the movie's central plot, which makes sense, seeing as the actor is the film's big celebrity "get." Pop singer Addison Rae, Nell Verlaque, Jalen Thomas Brooks (aka Mateo from "The Pitt"), and Milo Manheim round out the cast, with Gina Gershon joining them in a small role.

The Black Friday trampling might make one think that "Thanksgiving" is constructed as a pointed anti-shopping satire, lampooning the bizarreness that takes place at many retail outlets the day after Thanksgiving. Roth, however, is more keen on dark humor and gooey gore than anything thoughtful. That's more of his métier. However you feel about the filmmaker, he certainly knows the power of ridiculous kill scenes.

Again, critics generally accepted "Thanksgiving" as good, silly fun. Alonso Duralde and Christy Lemire, the co-hosts of the review show "Breakfast All Day," were both positive on the film, with Duralde saying that it makes for a great movie to see with a crowd and Lemire stressing that she had a great time. They both loved the film's cast, too, with Lemire admiring that, Addison Rae notwithstanding, it wasn't just famous person stunt casting. Eli Roth even commented on the movie's casting in an exclusive interview with /Film, so he is at least astute in that regard.

Reviewing "Thanksgiving" for IndieWire, Alison Foreman gave the film an A-, writing:

"With more time dedicated to table-setting than gorging on gore, Roth and [writer Jeff] Rendell create a living-breathing corner of the United States that's actually worth terrorizing. The people populating this fictional version of Plymouth don't feel 'real,' per se, but they're toxically relatable, and the complicated relationship they have with Thanksgiving is as politically charged as it is annoyingly pointless in life."

Foreman's only issue with "Thanksgiving" was that it relies very heavily on characters checking Instagram to tell its story, and it makes a lot of references to billionaire mogul Mark Cuban (!), meaning the film will feel incredibly dated very quickly. Although, she did enjoy the soundtrack, which includes bands like the Misfits and The Waitresses.

But then, all films become dated eventually, so that's hardly a cardinal sin. If you're looking for a fun Thanksgiving movie this holiday, and you are allergic to sentimentality, then maybe "Thanksgiving" will be more your beat.

Vitalik ‘pivots’ ETH to store of value, crypto suffers after US midterms: Hodler’s Digest

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Vitalik Buterin wants Ethereum to embrace a store of value narrative, upsetting Bitcoin maxis. The crypto industry faces an uncertain future following the US elections.

Ethereum creator Vitalik Buterin says the network should promote ETH as a store of value, rather than as an asset that derives value from burning fees.

He argued in a thread that idea of “ETH as fee-burning token never was as strong as it seemed” and that optimizing for that would negatively impact Ethereum’s “decentralization and capture resistance.”

Instead, he argues that Ethereum’s value should come from stability provided by its focus on “decentralization and CROPS” — referring to censorship and capture resistance, open source, privacy and security — which he believes will be more highly valued in an “era of AI disruption.”

The 2021 EIP-1559 fee burning upgrade flipped Ethereum into deflationary mode for a time when fee revenue was sky high, leading to supporters, including the Bankless podcasters, touting Ethereum as “ultrasound money.”

But due to design choices, fees have kept falling along with revenue, making that narrative unsustainable. From January to October this year median transaction fees fell from 35 cents to 3.5 cents, even as the number of daily transactions increased by more than half.

Some Bitcoiners, who hold BTC’s store of value status sacred, were scathing about Buterin’s thread. Held Media founder Dan Held derided Buterin’s comments as, “ETH trying to pivot to Bitcoin’s SoV narrative after all of its previous narratives collapsed. Again.” He argued it was “wild that you think a protocol with a constantly changing monetary policy (just like fiat) can be a ‘sound money.’” However, proponents note that Ethereum has not changed its monetary policy since The Merge in 2022, and each change has reduced, rather than increased, issuance.

Ethereum maxis welcomed the protocol’s creator setting a clear narrative direction. MetaLeX attorney Gabriel Shapiro called it a “nature is healing moment” from Buterin, and said, “I think on fundamentals ETH can be a much better SoV than both ZEC and BTC.”

Even Solana co-founder Anatoly Yakovenko said Buterin was “absolutely right that you can’t focus on fees as the product” although he then took a subtle dig at Ethereum’s “product.”

Despite spending $176 million across 100 seats — including $64 million trying and failing to keep Sherrod Brown out of the Senate — the influence of crypto super PACS on the election appeared to be minimal. Or was it?

Even though the Democrats now control both the Senate and the House of Representatives a Fairshake spokeman told Crypto In America the super PAC had spent $154.5 million across 93 races and backed the winning candidate in all but seven races. That’s a 92% success rate.

Stand With Crypto notes that a majority of legislators in both houses is now pro-crypto, with 50 of 100 senators designated “pro-crypto” and only 33 “anti-crypto,” while 241 congress people support the industry, and only 106 actively hate it.

However the anti-crypto army will slither into positions of power, with Brown likely to join the Senate Agriculture Committee and Elizabeth Warren tipped to chair the Senate Banking Committee.

“The next Congress will bring a fundamentally different environment for crypto in Washington, and the industry should expect significantly more scrutiny,” said Kyle Bligen, executive director at the Decentralization Research Center.Democrats are already reportedly considering expanding proposals from Representative Maxine Waters into a broader market structure bill. There is still an outside chance the CLARITY Act could be resurrected, but only if it has much stronger ethics provisions aimed at President Donald Trump’s alleged corruption.

“A sitting president’s family launching memecoins and a stablecoin is a real governance problem,” Fidelity Digital founder Hadley Stern told Magazine. “If I’d done that at Fidelity, I’d have been walked out the same afternoon.”

The TRUMP memecoin lost a third of its value this week, and was the biggest loser in the top 100 cryptocurrencies.

The election result saw AI related stocks crash and Bitcoin fell below $76,000 where it remains.

Binance reports that North Korean hackers first breached its systems back in June, when an employee downloaded a tampered Homebrew installation while attempting to install an AI coding tool. While the exchange quickly detected the incident, the hackers had already obtained information about employees working on Binance’s wallet infrastructure.

In September, attackers posing as recruiters targeted another software engineer and tricked the employee into downloading a malicious file disguised as a coding assignment.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used the compromised computer and an internal AI tool to gain access to two additional engineers’ systems to deploy malware. They then gained access to the exchange’s internal wallet-signing service, enabling them to steal billions.

“This wasn’t one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign,” Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said in a Tuesday post on X.

Ethereum Foundation researcher Justin Drake is targeting December 2028 to complete Ethereum’s post-quantum and post-AI transition, a year earlier than the foundation’s previously announced quantum-resistance deadline.

Speaking at Devcon 8 in Mumbai on Tuesday, Drake outlined the accelerated timeline. “Our hope is that by December 2028 we’ll be able to wrap up the full post-quantum and post-AI transition,” Drake said, adding that developers have 24 months to deliver three additional network upgrades.

The Ethereum Foundation’s September roadmap established December 2029 as its deadline for achieving quantum resistance.

However the roadmap is currently on hold, with core developers delaying the Glamsterdam upgrade indefinitely until security concerns tied to eight smart contract invariants are resolved.

THORChain has ignored the FBI’s explicit warning not to process funds from the Binance hack, with the permissionless protocol restoring trading on most networks on November 4. The RUNE token gained 15% in 24 hours on the news that Bitcoin, Ethereum, Bitcoin Cash, Dogecoin, Litecoin, Tron and Zcash had been re-enabled. Avalanche, Base and XRP were already operating.

Chainalysis reported that THORChain had already processed more than $46 million from the hack in the first 72 hours after Binance was attacked, with validators making around $200,000 in fees according to the Wall Street Journal. More would have undoubtedly gone through, but trading on the cross chain swaps platform was halted automatically due to an unrelated Ethereum block reorganization.

Co-founder JPThor said the fact that the FBI warning had no effect on the DEX proved that THORChain was decentralized.

At the end of the week, Bitcoin (BTC) is down 4.5% to trade at $74,302, Ethereum (ETH) is down 6.3% to trade at $2,117 and XRP (XRP) is down 5% to $1.11. The total market cap is at $2.49 trillion according to CoinMarketCap.

Among the biggest 100 cryptocurrencies, the top three altcoin winners of the week are Pieverse (PIEVERSE) with a 39.2% gain, Injective (INJ) on 11.1%, and Tether Gold (XAUt) on 4.2%.

The top three altcoin losers of the week are OFFICIAL TRUMP which was down 32.8%, Worldcoin (WLD) down 20.8% and Ethena (ENA) down 19%.

Binance’s $6.3 billion hack could temporarily hurt confidence in crypto markets but would ultimately lead to stronger security practices across exchanges, according to Grayscale.

“It also underscores the importance of institutional custody, such as offered by US-based crypto [exchange-traded products],” said Grayscale’s head of research, Zach Pandl, in a Tuesday report.

“These vehicles hold their assets at state-chartered banks and qualified custodians, typically offering offline storage, multi-signature wallets, SOC attestations and insurance policies,” he added.

Binance France is closing up shop in the country. All accounts will be closed on Dec. 31, and any assets left on the exchange will be charged monthly fees starting on Nov. 29, according to emails sent to users by Binance.

On that date, accounts still maintaining a balance will pay a monthly maintenance fee of 0.025% of the balance, or a minimum of 10 USDC, a stablecoin tracking the US dollar.

Cointelegraph reported in late June that Binance had notified European Union users access to key services would be restricted after the exchange failed to secure Markets in Crypto-Assets (MiCA) regulatory authorization from a member state before a July 1 deadline.

Suspicious prediction market bets were placed shortly before two recent New York Times/Siena political polls were released to the public, according to a report.

Vanderbilt University researchers said a Polymarket user bet almost $200,000 on Oct. 31 that Nebraska Senator Pete Ricketts would be re-elected. An hour later, a Times/Siena poll was released showing Ricketts in the lead.

A Friday New York Times report said while it was not clear if the user had access to inside information, they won more than $77,000. The user also placed similar large bets on other markets, including Kalshi, the report said.

Crypto-linked Visa debit card provider Gnosis Pay disclosed a security breach on Friday that exposed customers’ payment card details, home addresses and cryptocurrency wallet addresses.

Gnosis Pay said it detected unauthorized access to its legacy consumer card system on Nov. 3 and began containing the breach within two hours. The company said customer funds and its separate enterprise platform, which powers white-label card programs, were unaffected.

The CLARITY Act faces a new test as Democrats control Congress, raising questions over consumer protections, ethics and what the crypto industry may have to concede.

Bitcoin’s eCash fork gives eligible holders an equivalent balance of ECX, but questions remain over where they can trade the tokens and what they can do with them.

From setting up an SMSF or a company, to just leaving the country entirely, here are some strategies Australian Bitcoiners can employ to deal with 2027’s massive CGT hike.

Decentralized AI networks want to turn everyday phones into an alternative to centralized computing infrastructure — but skeptics have their doubts.

Rabiot fury at refereeing decision: ‘Lying won’t help, Italian football pays the consequences’

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Adrien Rabiot has raged against refereeing decisions after Milan’s 2-1 victory away to Genoa. ‘Mistakes happen, but lying continually won’t help you progress. Italian football suffers the consequences.’

The Rossoneri emerged 2-1 from the Stadio Luigi Ferraris this afternoon, thanks to the early goals from Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic.

However, they were far from happy at the final whistle, because Rabiot was booked for dissent and some of Ruben Amorim’s coaching staff red carded off the bench following an incident at the 50th minute.

Rabiot robbed defender Alessandro Marcandalli and was brought down in cynical fashion, so Milan wanted this to be a red card for DOGSO (Denial of a Goal Scoring Opportunity).

However, Marcandalli was only shown the yellow card, with the same punishment going to Rabiot for his protests.

It was evaluated by VAR and confirmed, but then former referee Gianpaolo Calvarese lit the fuse again with his Instagram explaining why he felt this was the correct decision.

“Rabiot’s chance was a scoring opportunity, but the rules state that in order for it to qualify as DOGSO, there have to be four parameters met,” said Calvarese.

“They are having possession of the ball, the general direction of the move, the number of defenders in the vicinity, and the distance from the goal. In this case, considering the foul took place towards the touchline and circa 60 metres from goal, I consider Davide Massa’s decision to show only a yellow card to be sustainable.

“At the same time, had Massa decided to show the red card, VAR would not have been able to intervene, so it remains a decision made on the pitch at the referee’s discretion.”

A post shared by Gianpaolo Calvarese (@gianpaolocalvarese_official)

This was a similar explanation to the one given by DAZN Italian’s refereeing expert, Luca Marelli, because the contact was inside the Milan half.

However, it did little to satisfy Rabiot, who replied with an ominous warning as a comment on the Instagram post.

“In life just as in football, you can make mistakes, but you should at least be able to admit them. Lying continually won’t help you progress. And it is Italian football that will pay the consequences.

Hes 100% right, bad officiating holds the whole league back, it doesnt anyone any favours.

Here’s the full text of Jensen Huang’s memo to Nvidia

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

In the memo Nvidia’s CEO acknowledges having an affair with a suspected Chinese spy.

In the memo Nvidia’s CEO acknowledges having an affair with a suspected Chinese spy.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

On Sunday, Nvidia announced it was launching an investigation into CEO Jensen Huang’s relationship with Alice Huang, an AI executive and agent of the Chinese government. In addition to the Board of Directors announcing the investigation, Huang sent a memo to Nvidia employees acknowledging the affair. You can read the full text below.

I owe my family and all of you an apology.

I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.

Throughout my life, my family has given me love and support. They deserve my honesty and loyalty. Instead, I let them down and damaged the life we built together. I take full responsibility for what I did and the hurt I caused, and I accept the criticism that comes with it.

To my wife and family: I love you. I know an apology cannot undo what I have done. I hope our family will heal and that, over time, I can earn back your trust.

To my colleagues at NVIDIA, including those I have worked alongside for decades, and to our board, partners, and shareholders around the world: you deserve better from me. Leading NVIDIA is the privilege of my life. My responsibility is to serve you and be worthy of your trust. I have fallen short of that responsibility. I am fully committed to NVIDIA and will work to earn back your confidence.

Regarding the national security concerns raised in news reports, NVIDIA’s Board of Directors has initiated an independent investigation. I support the investigation and will cooperate fully. I love our country and our company. I am committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America’s national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American.

My family bears no responsibility for my choices. I do. Please give them privacy.

Gary Neville issues Michael Carrick verdict with Manchester United sack decision imminent

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Gary Neville has given his verdict on Michael Carrick’s future as Manchester United manager after Sunday’s 4-4 draw with Aston Villa.

Carrick faces another international window filled with uncertainty after becoming the first Man Utd manager in 40 years to guide the club into the top-flight relegation zone.

Man Utd sit 18th with eight points after 10 matches, one point behind Coventry City, after a disjointed and rudderless performance at Old Trafford which somehow ended with a share of the spoils.

Alysson put Villa ahead after two minutes before Nicolas Jackson added a second two minutes later. However, Man Utd appeared to gain momentum when Bruno Fernandes pulled one back from the penalty spot, followed by Youri Tielemans levelling shortly before the break.

Man Utd again conceded twice in quick succession early in the second half to goals from John McGinn and Leon Goretzka. Joshua Zirkzee then offered the hosts a lifeline in stoppage time, before substitute Patrick Dorgu headed in the equaliser in the 96th minute.

Despite the late equaliser, Carrick remains on the brink. But Neville believes yet more change is inevitable but it is those further up the Man Utd food chain who should be at risk of the sack.

"Michael Carrick did that last season - so you can paper over the cracks and get a new manager bounce,” Neville told NBC Sports. "When the new manager comes in, everybody's happy for a couple of months and all of a sudden, six months later, it happens again.

"You can keep changing the manager and thinking that's going to be the answer. But this has been proven now over eight managers in 12 years, that's not the answer.

"Michael could leave, Michael might stay, we don't know, the ownership don't have a clue what to do.

"It's rinse and repeat. Amorim, Ten Hag, Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, Carrick, Jose Mourinho, Louis van Gaal, David Moyes, Ralf Rangnick - some of them are world class managers who have had success, there are a lot of promising young managers there, ex players who know the club as well.

"None of it has worked because if you recruit badly, if you've got poor performance department, poor recruitment department, you've not got leadership that is strong right through the spine of the football club you will fail.

Mauricio Pochettino favourite to be next Man Utd manager as Michael Carrick sack decision looms

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

A new name has stormed into favouritism to become the next Manchester United manager, with Michael Carrick on the brink of the sack.

Another barmy game saw Man Utd twice come from two goals down to earn a remarkable 4-4 draw with Aston Villa on Sunday, but the point was not enough to guide the club out of the relegation zone ahead of the international break.

As was widely reported, Carrick was given a two-game reprieve following the defeat at Chelsea last weekend, and has come away with a win over Roma and a draw with Villa. Neither performance could be considered remotely promising, so the pressure remains on his head.

But who will replace him if Carrick is sacked? Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann have been considered the leading contenders and remain there but are now joined by USA manager Mauricio Pochettino.

Pochettino has long been linked with the Old Trafford job, especially during his sucessful spell with Tottenham, which now ended seven years ago.

The Argentine recently signed a new contract to remain in charge of the USMNT, but is known to be open to a return to the Premier League.

The cheaper options of Howe and Nagelsmann may appeal more to Man Utd chiefs. Both are currently free agents after leaving Newcastle and the Germany national team respectively during the summer. Howe’s Premier League experience may be the deciding factor if a change is made.

According to reports, there is a difference of opinion in the Man Utd boardroom, with Howe the current preferred candidate of technical director James Wilcox, but Nagelsmann is highly regarded by Christopher Vivell, Man United’s head of recruitment.

Antonio Conte has made it clear that he would be interested in a return to English football, but Didier Deschamps, fresh from leaving the France national team setup, has reportedly dismissed an approach.

A new wildcard name in the frame is Stuttgart manager Sebastian Hoeness, who his appointment would be considered another big gamble.

Texans roll Chargers in first half, take 24-9 lead to locker room

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The Texans have figured out how to fix their defense: Play the Chargers.

Los Angeles has now gone 34 possessions without a touchdown. It has not reached the end zone since Kimani Vidal ran 42 yards for a score with 57 seconds remaining in the third quarter in an Oct. 11 loss to the Broncos.

The Texans limited the Chargers to 98 yards and held them to only three Cameron Dicker field goals of 52, 33 and 43 yards. They sacked Justin Herbert four times, including two by Sheldon Rankins.

Houston leads Los Angeles 24-9 at halftime.

Kayshon Boutte caught a 12-yard touchdown pass from C.J. Stroud with 31 seconds left in the half, completing a seven-play, 76-yard drive that might have been the deathknell for the Chargers.

The Texans have 267 yards, with Stroud going 15-of-24 for 230 yards with three touchdowns and an interception.

Texans wide receiver Tank Dell scored his first touchdown since the 2024 season, 687 days ago, with a 63-yard catch-and-run down the right sideline. Dell missed the 2025 season and six games this season while working his way back from a gruesome knee injury that ended his 2024 season.

Dell has two catches for 66 yards and Boutte two for 47. Woody Marks has 26 yards on seven carries.

Herbert is 9-of-17 for 63 yards, with one of his favorite wide receivers, Ladd McConkey, yet to play because of back spasms during pregame warmups.

Freiburg laud Yuito Suzuki’s resurgence after Japanese star breaks out of form slump

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

SC Freiburg head coach Julian Schuster has revealed the extensive work undertaken by his coaching staff to help Japanese international Yuito Suzuki overcome a difficult spell. The 25-year-old responded with an outstanding performance in Sunday’s 2-0 Bundesliga victory over Union Berlin, scoring his fifth league goal of the season and earning Get German Football News‘ Player of the Match honours.

Suzuki’s 67th-minute finish, calmly lifted over onrushing Union keeper Frederik Rønnow following a high recovery and pass from Derry Scherhant, supplied the crowning moment of an industrious performance. The Japanese international had threatened on several previous occasions and even initially received credit for Freiburg’s 55th-minute opener before replays established that Rani Khedira had turned Jan-Niklas Beste’s ball into his own net.

The goal ended a frustrating period for a player who had begun the 2026/27 campaign in sensational fashion. More importantly, Schuster’s post-match comments revealed that Suzuki’s return to form was the product of considerable attention from Freiburg’s coaching staff.

Those following Suzuki’s development will recall that Get German Football News profiled the Japanese international’s physical and technical progression shortly before October’s international break. Suzuki had opened the Bundesliga campaign with a hat-trick against Werder Bremen and subsequently scored an excellent volley in Freiburg’s 2-2 draw with Eintracht Frankfurt.

Having overcome considerable adaptation difficulties during his first season in Breisgau, the former Brøndby IF attacker appeared ready to assume greater responsibility following Johan Manzambi’s departure to Aston Villa. The initial progress nevertheless gave way to a difficult spell. Suzuki’s unfortunate dismissal against Schalke 04 on matchday five interrupted his momentum.

Schuster criticized his player’s loss of composure after the match, even as the Freiburg trainer praised the collective response that enabled his ten-man side to secure a dramatic 2-1 victory. Suzuki subsequently struggled to rediscover his earlier attacking influence. Meanwhile, fellow Japanese international Keisuke Goto continued to make a compelling case for additional playing time. The 21-year-old followed up his late matchwinner against Schalke with another decisive substitute appearance in Freiburg’s DFB-Pokal victory over Wolfsburg.

In a detailed November 3rd Kicker report, veteran German footballing correspondent Carsten Schröter-Lorenzexamined the Japanese international’s declining influence and spoke with SCF sporting director Klemens Hartenbach. The statistical findings were noteworthy. Suzuki had averaged a disappointing Kicker rating of 4.7 across five competitive fixtures. His involvement in possession had declined considerably, with his touches per 90 minutes falling below 40 against Jablonec and remaining unusually low in subsequent appearances. His duel statistics had also deteriorated.

Interestingly, the running data suggested that Suzuki’s problems could not simply be attributed to a lack of effort. In several matches, his distance covered and sprint figures had actually improved compared with the opening weeks of the season. Hartenbach instead pointed toward the expectations Suzuki faced following his spectacular start. The sporting director explained that the player had been frustrated by what he considered insufficient playing time with the Japanese national team. After enjoying tremendous support in Freiburg, Suzuki had struggled to maintain the same carefree approach.

Hartenbach described a player whose mind had begun racing and who found it increasingly difficult to recover when relatively simple actions went wrong. Hartenbach noted that Suzuki lived from his spontaneous creativity and found himself overthinking touches. The sporting director’s prescription was revealing. Suzuki needed to return to his high-volume running game, defensive commitment, and willingness to regain possession rather than focus excessively on producing goals and assists.

Five days later, Schuster would explain how Freiburg had attempted to put precisely that approach into practice.

Speaking at Sunday’s post-match press conference, Schuster was asked directly about Suzuki’s recent difficulties by Kicker journalist Daniela Frahm. Frahm referred to the player’s dissatisfaction with his own performances and questioned the Freiburg coach about the work undertaken to help him overcome the slump.

“Yes, he did [voice dissatisfaction with himself],” Schuster responded. “There were more than a few conversations, also with some of my assistant coaches. There’s been a lot of solid work invested to ensure that no one gets left behind.”

The Freiburg trainer acknowledged that Suzuki’s earlier success and his subsequent involvement with Japan had brought challenges that were not always easy for a young player to process.

“I think it was logical [from him],” Schuster continued. “We got off to a good start this year, then Yuito – like many of the other lads – was on tour with the national team. One hears things. Things get into one’s head that aren’t easy for a young person to deal with.”

“If one wishes to be successful in the long run, it’s important to keep it relative and remain grounded,” Schuster noted. “It’s important to remain grateful and constantly remind yourself that success can come both as an individual and as a collective. And to realize where development and progress comes from. It’s totally normal and logical that careers and progress aren’t a constant upward trend. Everyone experiences occasional dips. In such times, it’s important to not focus on goals or assists, but the basics that can help deliver a good performance.”

Schuster’s concluding remarks offered the clearest insight into how Freiburg had prepared Suzuki for Sunday’s match.

“That was Yuito’s specific instruction today,” Schuster concluded. “It was to concentrate on that solely. To work against the ball, to raise his intensity to the point that he could physically and mentally compete. To simply use his vision to make the right moves on the ball, initiate the right contacts, and try to look for the right depth. He did that today and it’s very important that he delivered such a performance today.”

The tactical instructions closely resemble the qualities Schuster had already praised earlier in the season. As noted in GGFN’s October player check, the Freiburg coach was particularly pleased with Suzuki’s growing appreciation of the defensive responsibilities expected of an attacking midfielder.

Sunday’s performance supplied further evidence that the lessons had not been forgotten.

Suzuki covered 10.53 kilometres, registered 25 sprints, and reached a top speed of 32.9 km/h against Union. He recorded 44 touches, attempted three shots, and consistently sought opportunities to attack the spaces opening up around the visitors’ defensive structure.

The goal itself was important. The response to the coaching staff’s instructions may prove considerably more significant.

After a month in which Suzuki’s form and starting position had increasingly come under discussion, Schuster’s assessment suggested that the Japanese international had taken an important step toward rediscovering his confidence.

Hartenbach had identified the necessary ingredients five days earlier. Against Union Berlin, Suzuki finally brought them together again.

Maya Jama Steps Down As Love Island UK Host After 9 Seasons

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Love Island UK host Maya Jama announced she’s exiting the dating reality series after nine seasons, sharing the news in a lengthy statement: “All good things must come to an end.”

The most iconic bombshell is leaving the Villa.

Love Island UK host Maya Jama revealed she’s decided not to return to the dating series after nine seasons, reflecting on her journey on the ITV reality show in a heartfelt statement.

“What an incredible era and what an unforgettable time I’ve had, it’s been far too much fun to put into words or a video,” the British TV presenter wrote in a Nov. 8 Instagram post, “but as all good things must come to an end, it’s my time to step down as the host of my beloved Love Island.”

Alongside a video compilation of memorable moments over the years, Jama—who served as host on the ITV series and the companion show Love Island Aftersun, along with spinoffs Love Island Games and Love Island: All-Stars—expressed her gratitude toward those who supported her throughout her experience.

“I want to thank all of the people who rooted so hard for me to host the show in the first place. I wouldn’t change a thing,” she wrote. “To the fans of the show, thank you for every supportive message and every single time you’ve tuned in during my time as host, I’m forever grateful to you all.”

Jama added, “The show really does belong to the people who watch it and embrace it year after year.”

After thanking the “incredible production team,” the 32-year-old—who took over hosting duties in 2023 after Laura Whitmore’s exit—shared insight into her years with the “iconic” reality TV show franchise.

“I started hosting the show aged 28 and during the 9 incredible series met the most wonderful humans along the way,” Jama recalled. “I’ve loved every slow-mo walk, fire pit and live show. I’ll forever be grateful for the experience.”

While the Celebrity Traitors star didn’t go into detail about her decision to step away from Love Island, she gave a glimpse at her mindset.

“I’ve always believed that to truly grow, you have to take a leap every now and then,” she wrote, “and now is my time to throw myself into something new.”

Jama concluded her goodbye message with a touching sentiment, emphasizing that she’ll continue to be a fan.

“While my own Love Island journey has come to an end,” she wrote, “I wish the show and the team all the success in the world, I’ll be cheering you lot on from the sidelines forever.”

The Gentlemen actress summarized her thoughts while reposting her Reel on her Instagram Stories, writing, “I feel emotional,” adding that it's an “end of an era.”

Which Bitcoin Treasury Stocks Could Surge on Bullish CPI Data Drop?

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Bitcoin treasury stocks are set to react sharply to Tuesday’s US CPI release, as these companies’ share prices track Bitcoin closely and Bitcoin has grown more sensitive to inflation data as a macro asset.

The Consumer Price Index (CPI), published by the Bureau of Labor Statistics, measures the monthly change in prices paid by urban consumers for a fixed basket of goods and services. Headline CPI includes food and energy; core CPI excludes those volatile categories.

Markets and the Federal Reserve watch it closely because sustained readings above the Fed’s roughly 2% target influence rate decisions, liquidity, and risk appetite. The October 2026 data drops on Tuesday, November 10, at 8:30 a.m. ET.

Key Events This Week:1. October CPI Inflation data – Tuesday 2. OPEC Monthly Report – Wednesday3. Initial Jobless Claims data – Thursday4. October Existing Home Sales data – Thursday5. October PPI Inflation data – Friday 6. Total of 17 Fed Speaker Events This WeekWe…

Consensus forecasts point to a cooling print: headline CPI is expected around +0.2% to +0.3% month-over-month (versus +0.6% in September), with core also near +0.2%.

Year-over-year figures may be limited by prior data gaps, but models such as the Cleveland Fed’s nowcast project roughly 3.7% headline and low 2.5% core. Energy moderation and softer goods prices are cited as drivers.

A print in line with or below these expectations would likely be viewed as dovish, supporting expectations of easier policy or delayed further tightening. That environment typically lifts Bitcoin (and risk assets more broadly) by reducing the opportunity cost of holding non-yielding assets and improving liquidity conditions.

Bitcoin has shown heightened CPI sensitivity since the 2022 hiking cycle; cooler-than-expected data has often produced positive short-term moves, while hotter prints have triggered quick sell-offs.

Treasury stocks, whose market values are heavily tied to Bitcoin holdings, amplify those swings. A hotter-than-expected number could reverse the dynamic, pressuring Bitcoin and the equities that hold it.

Sunday homework: the Street’s forecast board.Fed seen hiking to 4.25% in December, peaking near 4.34% in Q1 2027, then cutting below 4% by 2028.CPI peaks at 3.8% this quarter. Payrolls slow to ~60K a month. Recession odds 20%.The 2-year trades 4.49% vs a 4.67% forecast. The… pic.twitter.com/bPQrXYoSEy

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Strategy (MSTR) remains the largest Bitcoin treasury proxy, holding about 849,000 BTC. Recently trading near $129, it shows a short-term bearish bias with an RSI of 40–45. Support is near $125–$126, with stronger support around $120, while resistance is at $131–$134. A positive CPI could drive a relief rally to $131–$135; conversely, a negative CPI might test the $120–$125 support band.

Mara Holdings (MARA), a major miner holding over 35,000 BTC, trades around $6.30–$6.35. Its technicals are mixed to bearish, with an RSI near 34. Immediate support is at $6.20–$5.90, while resistance is at $6.50–$6.80. A positive CPI could lead to a bounce towards $6.80–$7.00; a negative print may drop it to the mid-$5 range.

Strive (AAST) holds over 40,000 BTC and recently traded at $21.45, showing mixed signals as its RSI has fluctuated between the high-30s and mid-60s. Support is in the high teens to low $20s, with resistance in the mid-to-upper $20s. It is likely to track Bitcoin closely, with a dovish CPI potentially pushing it higher.

Overall, these stocks are more volatile than Bitcoin itself, so traders should watch the CPI deviation from consensus and Bitcoin’s reaction for confirmation.

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Week 2 of Fortnite Treasure Hunters has added the Lil’ Uzi Vert Sprite to the existing collection, and we can add it to our collection. To do so, however, we have to first collect this Sprite on our runs, and then extract it safely using an Extractor.

Let’s find out how it works, and how to get it.

Unlike most Sprites in the current season, the Lil’ Uzi Vert Jr. Sprite is a guaranteed drop. However, you’ll have to raid Lil’ Uzi Vert Jr.’s stronghold, and outlast the boss battle.

Thankfully, he is one of the easiest bosses to take down. His weapon might do a lot of damage, but not so much if you stay in the shelter and shoot from a distance. Just go for headshots, and you’ll be good to go.

Once you defeat him, you’ll get one of the three possible variants of the Lil’ Uzi Vert Jr. Sprite. The base version is more common, and that’s the one you’ll be mostly receiving. However, you can also end up finding the Cursed or the Fortune variants if you’re lucky enough. The special versions have the same effect as the base version, but they offer an extra modifier.

The Lil’ Uzi Vert Jr. Sprite helps to restore shields of players by triggering a splash effect. This will heal you and your allies. While the move has a cooldown, the time interval can come down once you level up the Sprite. This makes the Sprite one of the best options to consider for team play.

Taylor Swift is no stranger to attending a Kansas City Chiefs game. (Usually wearing thematic red and white merch, no less.) But tonight, as the Chiefs faced off against the New York Jets at Kansas City’s Arrowhead Stadium, Swift brought along a special friend to spectate the exciting match: Her longtime friend, Karlie Kloss.

The pop singer was spotted wearing a red sweater (go Chiefs!) and a statement necklace in her private box, while sitting beside Kloss and her parents, Andrea and Scott Swift. Swift and Kloss have been close friends since the early 2010s—and they even shared a Vogue cover together in 2015—though more recently, they have been rarely photographed together. (There have been rumors for years of a brief rift between the two friends, though neither party has ever confirmed this.)

The friend link-up at the game may seem like the first in a long time, but back in July, Kloss was one of the special guests at Swift and Kelce’s wedding at Madison Square Garden. “Listen, I try to protect the privacy of my friends’ personal lives,” Kloss later said of the grand event. “But what I will say is that they’re an amazing couple and I’m so happy for them.”

It makes sense that Swift was on-hand at tonight’s game, and brought along a special friend for the occasion. There was lots to celebrate, after all, given the game marked Kelce’s 200th match in the NFL. So, did you really think Swift would miss such a milestone? For every Taylor Swift concert that Kelce shows up for, the singer will be in the box, dressed in red with a best pal.

England’s Jordan Pickford Named the ‘Toughest’ Striker He’s Faced

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Everton and England goalkeeper Jordan Pickford has named the toughest striker he's ever come up against.

Pickford is currently enjoying a fruitful start to the new season with the Toffees, keeping six clean sheets across all competitions thus far. While the Merseysiders are unlikely to qualify for the Champions League, the shot-stopper will certainly harbour hopes of qualifying for European competition of some kind as David Moyes' well-oiled outfit continue to improve.

Despite his impressive exploits for England at major tournaments, Pickford is yet to play at the very highest level for his club, meaning he's only come up against the continent's best footballers on a handful of occasions.

Regardless, the number one has still faced off against the likes of Lionel Messi, Cristiano Ronaldo and Kylian Mbappe throughout his career for the Three Lions. None of these aforementioned three was cited by the goalkeeper himself as the best he's ever faced.

Speaking to Everton's official YouTube Channel back in 2024, Pickford was asked who he feels is the toughest player he's ever played against. In typically loyal fashion, the ex-Sunderland man said Harry Kane.

“Toughest opponent I've faced?" he pondered.

"It’s hard as a goalkeeper, that. As a finisher, I’d say Harry Kane.”

This claim was made two years before Pickford took on Messi, although it was also two years prior to Kane winning the prestigious Ballon d'Or award.

The England striker has scored 543 goals in 661 career games for club and country, so it's not a huge surprise that Pickford selected the ridiculously prolific goalscorer, particularly from his perspective as a goalkeeper.

The shot-stopper, who has over 90 caps for his country, has trained with Kane for years at St. George's Park, and will be well aware of the Bayern Munich forward's quality and prowess.

The number nine also has 16 career goals against Everton, many of which will have come against Pickford, who probably had little chance of saving any of them.

As shown by the table above, Kane hasn't just enjoyed goal-scoring success against the Toffees — his team has often come out on top against them.

The United Kingdom has produced some legendary forwards throughout the years.

Thus, having both conceded and lost to the former Tottenham star throughout his career, it makes sense that the player comes to Pickford's mind in a snapshot interview about the toughest he's ever faced, over some of the other world-class stars he's had much more limited experience with.

Interestingly, there have been reports in recent months that Bayern are eyeing a move for Pickford, so he could soon be playing alongside Kane for both club and country.

Book Review: The Psychology of AI Adoption at Work: From Resistance to Results by Gleb Tsipursky

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Even running a news website on AI, I ran into internal resistance from employees who were reluctant to adopt AI fully. While we used AI for market research, bullet-point summarization, and translations, there was hesitation about fully deploying agentic AI: research agents that would discover news and report on it autonomously. It was only by pushing ahead despite those objections, while working to mitigate their concerns, that we were able to deploy our fleet of autonomous AI agents. That experience is what drew me to The Psychology of AI Adoption at Work: From Resistance to Results by Gleb Tsipursky.

In this book, I hoped to unlock the mystery behind why so many employees fear fully adopting AI, and how to encourage those team members to simply explore how powerful AI can be in completely revolutionizing their way of working.

The book draws from various studies and reports to demonstrate the potential for AI to improve productivity and enable business growth. This is, of course, nothing magical for anyone who works with AI on a regular basis, especially when it comes to vibe coding software applications. The ability to move from an idea to a working application can change what a small team is capable of achieving.

This book targets executives who need to motivate and convince their staff to adopt AI before the company is left behind by a competitor who does, all while fighting the familiar urge to maintain the status quo.

The book walks us through various case studies that make an increasingly convincing argument for adoption. It is only then that we learn about the human psychological traits and barriers that make full company-wide adoption difficult to achieve. Seeing the potential of AI and convincing an entire organization to act on it are two very different challenges.

Hidden fears, biases, and emotional triggers such as automation anxiety can undermine AI adoption. These issues are then amplified by organizational cultures that are sometimes misaligned with the best of intentions.

Part of the issue is that people are hesitant to accept new technologies when they are powered by what is essentially a black box model. A black box AI model produces outputs from inputs, but its internal decision-making process is difficult for humans to understand or explain. This is one reason explainable AI matters: people want a basis for understanding and evaluating the systems they are being asked to trust.

One of the most discussed biases is status quo bias, the tendency to favor keeping things as they are. In the workplace, this can be reinforced by an exaggerated focus on the perceived risks of change. Employees who have repeated the same process or used legacy software for years develop routines and a comfort zone that risks being shattered. Founders and leaders are excited to share the transformative impact of reimagining their companies and processes with AI, but are often puzzled when they are met with caution, resistance, and sometimes outright defiance.

Other fears that need to be tackled include job displacement, often described as automation anxiety. Staff fear that if they begin to adopt AI tools, eventually those tools will replace them. It is not always easy to explain that refusing to learn these tools can also create a competitive disadvantage against people who understand their potential and change their workflows to incorporate them. Leaders still need to be honest about how roles may change and give employees a practical opportunity to learn.

Confirmation bias is another cognitive bias that can cause rejection of AI. Humans naturally gravitate toward social media posts, news, or other information that confirms what they already believe. A bias against AI can then be reinforced through groupthink and conversations with other employees who are equally reluctant to adopt the technology.

The same tendency can affect those of us who are enthusiastic about AI. If we want employees to evaluate these tools fairly, we also need to remain willing to examine the failures alongside the successes.

Managers often play a dual role. While they are responsible for implementing corporate objectives, they also have to balance those objectives with the concerns of staff who look to them for guidance on job security and procedural changes.

The book describes five profiles of middle managers:

The book then digs into eight psychographic profiles of employees. Learning how to engage with managers and team members who have different personalities is what this book is fundamentally about. As a business owner, how do you acknowledge concerns, mitigate risks, and change the corporate culture to enable AI adoption?

This is what The Psychology of AI Adoption at Work: From Resistance to Results does well. It gives founders and executives a way to understand the resistance that can seem so puzzling when the benefits of AI are already obvious to them. For someone like me, who is already convinced of AI’s transformative potential, the value is in learning how to bring more of the team along. The opportunity is enormous, but turning that opportunity into company-wide adoption requires understanding the people whose way of working you are asking to change. I would recommend this book to business owners and leaders who are ready to move ahead with AI and want to build a culture that is ready to move with them.

Antoine is a visionary leader and founding partner of Unite.AI, driven by an unwavering passion for shaping and promoting the future of AI and robotics. A serial entrepreneur, he believes that AI will be as disruptive to society as electricity, and is often caught raving about the potential of disruptive technologies and AGI.

As a futurist, he is dedicated to exploring how these innovations will shape our world. In addition, he is the founder of Securities.io, a platform focused on investing in cutting-edge technologies that are redefining the future and reshaping entire sectors.

Vanoli: ‘Fiorentina ought to feel angry at 2-2 Juventus draw’

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Paolo Vanoli insists Fiorentina ‘ought to feel angry’ they only drew 2-2 with Juventus after leading twice, but this team ‘is on the right path and it was a strong signal.’

The Viola had started to dream of beating their bitter rivals at the Stadio Artemio Franchi, especially after taking the lead in the opening four minutes with a Mateo Pellegrino header.

However, Nick Woltemade drew level, and when Alieu Njie bundled in at the back post, that too was cancelled out by the Gleison Bremer header on a corner.

Radu Dragusin had the ball in the net for a third time, but it was disallowed for the defender’s push on Bremer.

“I think we had a great performance against a strong opponent,” Vanoli told DAZN Italia.

“We honoured what our fans asked for, and it must disappoint us that we did not get the full three points. These are young players and we ought to give them more confidence, I think we played well today, and also know there is a lot of work to be done.”

There were some surprising choices in the starting XI, bringing in Pedro Goncalves and Christ Inao Oulai rather than Marco Brescianini and Niccolò Fagioli.

“Before kick-off, people said my line-up was courageous. If we want to play football, then it’s not about using players who are more defensive or more offensive, it’s about everyone working hard and chasing down opponents,” added Vanoli.

“I think today these lads did that. I feel this is the right path, we need to do better and have more determination on the ball, or when winning it back, which is something we still don’t do well enough. These are things we need to improve, but it was still a strong signal.

“My players ought to be angry, because even if they are young and inexperienced, they should be upset at putting in such a great performance and not winning the game. We all expect more.”

Pellegrino was on target today, but has seemed at times a little isolated in attack when Fiorentina play.

“We are trying to give him better service, but above all get closer to him, as we tend to give him the ball and then aren’t ready for when he holds it up. Franco Mastantuono also needs to come between the lines and dialogue with Pellegrino, which Pedro Goncalves does better because he is more accustomed to that cut inside.

“This is a team that wants to do something important, we are on the right track and must keep working on it,” confessed Vanoli.

Fiorentina have at least managed to scramble their way out of the relegation zone with a three-round unbeaten run, but are only two points clear of Monza.

This also extends their positive streak against Juventus, managing two wins and three stalemates since the 1-0 defeat in April 2024.

Soul Glo’s Diaspora Problems is a hardcore record like no other

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Diaspora Problems is one of the 21st century’s best punk albums.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Diaspora Problems is one of the 21st century’s best punk albums.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Soul Glo has a new record called NA I’M GON GRAB ALLAT coming out in January, and I, for one, can not wait. While two singles have dropped, it’s not enough to truly tide me over, so I’ve been revisiting Songs to Yeet at the Sun and my favorite album of 2022 — Diaspora Problems.

“Gold Chain Punk (whogonbeatmyass?)” is an opening salvo for the ages. Bong rip, into a catchy-as-hell hardcore riff, with frontman Pierce Jordan ranting. Then comes the half-time stomp as Jordan gets fully unhinged, screaming “Who gon beat my ass? Who the fuck gon beat my ass?” From here on out, as the music shifts through a few different sections, the energy never comes down. Jordan shreds his vocal cords, begging, “Can I live?” Before finally spelling it out for anyone still confused:

Say more, say less, it’s all the sameWhen it’s time to die we can meet outsideTo see who gon feel my pain

The record barely lets up from here on out. “Coming Correct” opens with a cough and a steady 808, giving you literally three seconds to catch your breath before Jordan jumps on the mic shouting, “I try to listen the way, I wanna be listened to.” The song starts stacking elements quickly, reaching full firestorm by 25 seconds in, and then doesn’t let up for the rest of its three-minute runtime.

The front chunk of the record is just perfect unrelenting hardcore song after perfect unrelenting hardcore song. Jordan’s crazed charisma is undeniable as he ricochets between leftist rhetoric, goofy bits about smoking weed, and vulnerable explorations of his struggles with depression. You’d be forgiven for struggling to keep up with his rapid-fire delivery, which owes almost as much to modern rappers as it does to classic hardcore acts like Minor Threat or Bad Brains.

Track six, “Driponomics,” fully embraces those more modern influences with trap snares, wall-rattling 808 kicks, and a guest verse from Mother Maryrose. The sampled guitar calls to mind the sax squeals favored by the Bomb Squad… just heavier.

The album eventually culminates in “Spiritual Level Of Gang Shit,” which features guest verses from McKinley Dixon and Lojii over gently echoed guitar melodies and dub-inspired groove. Then, around the 2:20 mark, things take a turn as the guitars turn punishing and the drums shift to a standard hardcore beat. But the song hasn’t reached its final form yet. That comes almost a minute later when the horn section kicks in.

Next to other modern hardcore standard bearers like Turnstile, Soul Glo might almost seem like traditionalists heavily influenced by the DC scene. But Diaspora Problems shows their ambition and willingness to take risks. Soul Glo plays with genre, is confrontational in its politics, and is generally just uncompromising.

Oh, and they put on a hell of a live show (I’ve seen them at least four times now). Don’t miss their incredible Tiny Desk Concert either, which I’m pretty sure is the first mosh pit to even open up in the NPR offices.

Soul Glo’s Diaspora Problems is available on Bandcamp and most major streaming services, including Qobuz, Deezer, YouTube Music, Apple Music, and Spotify.

The Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus v2 Red Cross Edition — CleanTechnica Tested

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Jackery was one of the earliest portable power station producers to bring these beautiful creations into the mainstream. They have a line of portable power stations and proprietary portable, foldable solar panels that continues to make them one of the top go-to options for anyone looking for power on the go.

Recently, they launched a Red Cross Edition of their popular HomePower 2000 Plus v2 that donates a portion of the profits to the Red Cross to support first responders. We covered these limited edition units when they first hit our radar during our visit to Jackery’s headquarters in Shenzhen, China, and now they are finally moving into production.

Jackery donates 10% of profits from this series to donate portable power stations to rescue organizations and emergency response teams, supporting disaster relief and recovery efforts. At the volumes Jackery is producing, that’s a meaningful step in the right direction to putting portable power stations into the hands of the first responders who need them most.

Disclaimer: Jackery provided the Red Cross edition of the 2000 Pro V2 portable power station to the author for the purposes of this review.

Charging up the unit is a simple matter thanks to its ability to pull a charge from multiple sources. Right out of the gate, you can charge from a normal 120V AC wall outlet at around 1,600 watts. This is typically the maximum you can safely charge from any wall outlet without overheating cables or outlets, and the Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus V2 makes full use of it. In our testing, we pulled 1,590W from the wall for a full charge from empty in an hour and a half.

You can also charge up the unit with solar panels, which with 800 watts of solar can recharge the battery from fully drained to overflowing in 3.2 hours. Jackery uses a proprietary solar connector, so keep that in mind before you buy your battery and/or panels.

You can also charge it up via your car’s 12V automotive outlet (requires optional car charging adapter for $29.90), which can push power out at about 100 watts. This varies slightly from car to car, but the fact of the matter is that you’re generally limited based on the factory wiring for what that outlet can handle.

Jackery knew about that, so they developed their own DC-to-DC charger for combustion vehicles. This optional accessory costs $239, but taps into the full power of your alternator and can pull up to 600 watts. This is a faster charge and can top up the battery in about 4 hours, but requires a bit of extra planning and work and assumes you’re running a combustion vehicle. It’s worth consulting with a professional if you’re going to go this route, as alternator wiring and max output varies greatly from car to car.

Electric vehicles typically don’t have a 12V outlet, opting for USB-C instead. Unfortunately, the Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus V2 Red Cross Edition is not able to charge up via USB-C at any power level. Fortunately, many electric cars come with integrated AC outlets, which can be used to charge the battery up in a pinch.

I tested this in my Cybertruck and achieved similar charging rates to what I was able to achieve at home, which tops the battery up in about an hour and a half. This is a great option to use while fast charging on a road trip because you can charge for about 30 minutes at a time to keep your portable power station charged up without having to run the inverter while driving, which drains the battery faster.

When it’s time to tap into all that stored power, the Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus v2 Red Cross Edition is ready. It can push out 2,400 watts of continuous power with surge capability of up to 4,800W. Given that this is a compact 2 kWh unit weighing just 21 lb, that’s a pretty solid level of power output that it can handle. Power output has continuously been improving with each successive generation and Jackery continues to be near the top of the pack with its numbers.

It’s obviously up to the task of powering lightweight items like drones, laptops, portable lights, charging radios, keeping the internet on, and even powering your fridge in an emergency. On the home front, it’s also a great option. Thanks to its LFP chemistry, Jackery expects you to get 6,000 charging cycles out of this battery, which equates to more than 16 years of daily cycling.

Even though it is such a compact unit, it should be able to keep essentials running at home — including your refrigerator, freezer, computer, lights, smartphone, Wi-Fi, etc. — for nearly 10 hours. That should get you through most outages, and, honestly, if you’re having longer outages than that, you probably want to look into a home battery system. Or you can tap into the HomePower 2000 Plus v2 Red Cross Edition’s ace up its sleeve….

The Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus V2 Red Cross edition can actually add more capacity. It has a special port on the left side of the unit that lets you plug in any of Jackery’s Battery Plus expansion batteries for up to 12kWh of total capacity. That’s a 6× increase over this unit’s installed capacity and lets you scale your storage capacity as you find new use cases for it.

The use case for this at home is really keeping the lights and and the refrigerator running during an emergency and using solar or a generator to charge up when it runs low. It can absorb up to 800 watts of solar from an attached array, meaning you can fully charge it in about an hour and a half depending on the sunlight you’re getting. Even in terrible conditions, it’s still going to charge up in just a couple of hours.

We put this beast to use running a portable refrigerator, lights, USB, charging our laptop and phones, and even charging a few portable batteries for us to take with us hiking. This unit kept our devices charged up for several days of fun in the desert. It was great knowing that after we were done charging our devices, the battery would top itself up again from the attached solar panels.

No matter how many devices we plugged in, including our usual kitchen torture test, its 2,400W of power output was more than enough to keep everything running. The surge power is pretty cool too because it allows the unit to absorb the spikes in consumption that come from a lot of devices when you first turn them on.

But we wanted more. So we pulled out some new winter heaters and really made the Jackery sweat. Each of these heating units can pull up to 1,500 watts each, which is about the max you can get from a standard 15A outlet. We plugged both into the Jackery and cranked them up to the max. I’m personally thrilled to have more energy-hungry appliances to torture — I mean test — portable power stations with.

Playing with all of their settings, we had them pulling almost 3,100 watts constantly before the Jackery finally decided to throw in the towel. It kept them running for several minutes over its own 2,400 watt constant power output rating, which was impressive, especially considering how compact this thing is.

It’s exciting to see Jackery continuing to push into new battery technologies, but in this case, this is the same battery they sell as a normal unit, just rebranded to highlight the great work being done at the Red Cross and to give some of the profits back in the form of product.

That’s worth celebrating, and it’s exciting to see Jackery leading the charge to enable first responders with technology without charging governments or consumers more money for the same product.

Or maybe you just like a bright red unit that’ll make it hard to lose or forget. Whether you’re heading out on a camping trip or heading back to the house after a long week out in nature, the bright red colorway of this unit is sure to stand out.

For more information about the Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus Red Cross Edition or to purchase one for yourself, head over to Jackery’s official website.

Live intentionally, make conscious decisions, love more, act responsibly, play. The more you know, the less you need. As an activist investor, Kyle owns long term stock holdings in Tesla and Rivian.

Kakao Mobility Signs NVIDIA MOU for Level 4 Autonomous Driving

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Kakao Mobility announced on November 9, 2026, that it had signed a memorandum of understanding with NVIDIA to develop Level 4 autonomous-driving technology, initially combining their systems on Kia purpose-built vehicles (PBVs).

The planned work pairs Kakao Mobility’s autonomous-driving AI and mobility platform with NVIDIA’s computing, sensing and safety technology. The stated goal is a Level 4 system aligned with global standards, building on urban driving that Kakao Mobility says has validated its technology in Seoul’s Gangnam district.

Kakao Mobility is progressively developing an end-to-end autonomous-driving model that connects perception through decision-making in one model. It plans to introduce an autonomous-driving computing unit containing two NVIDIA DRIVE AGX Thor chips, intended to process large-scale AI computation in real time. That hardware plan connects the model’s development directly to the compute capacity required to run it in a vehicle.

NVIDIA describes Hyperion as a standardized reference architecture integrating compute, sensors, networking and safety systems, with DRIVE AGX Thor forming part of the platform. Kakao Mobility intends to use the architecture to optimize connections among the vehicle, sensors and computing unit while pursuing system stability and compatibility.

The safety work extends into software development and validation through NVIDIA Halos. NVIDIA describes Halos OS as a three-layer safety architecture built on ASIL D-certified DriveOS foundations, integrating safety middleware and deployable safety applications. Together, Hyperion and Halos supply the system architecture and safety framework around Kakao Mobility’s own driving software.

The companies will prioritize integrated development on Kia PBVs under Kakao Mobility’s July 2026 partnership with Kia to develop PBVs for autonomous-driving services. They identify the Kia PV5 as the initial model for the combined technology. The plan is to develop and supply vehicles for Level 4 services using Kakao Mobility’s software alongside NVIDIA’s AI computing and safety systems.

That gives the agreement a specific integration path: Kakao Mobility contributes the driving software and mobility platform, NVIDIA is to contribute computing and safety technology, and the Kia PBV is the first vehicle target. Commercialization remains an objective of that development work.

Kakao Mobility CEO Ryu Gung-seon said the collaboration is intended to accelerate end-to-end development and prepare for Level 4 commercialization through an ecosystem spanning software, components and finished vehicles. NVIDIA automotive vice president Rishi Dhall framed the nearer objective as accelerating safe and scalable autonomous-driving services in South Korea.

The companies are also leaving open medium- to long-term global expansion, based on technology validated in South Korea. The immediate test is integration on the PV5: turning the separate software, compute, safety and vehicle contributions into a validated Level 4 system is the next step toward the commercial driverless service described in the announcement.

From the Archives: When Iowa Governor-Elect Rob Sand Was Shot by Steven Meisel for Vogue Italia

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

“Be My Baby,” by Steven Meisel, was originally published in the February 2004 issue of Vogue Italia.

For more of the best from Vogue’s archive, sign up for our Nostalgia newsletter here.

With a fluffed-up quiff and a supermodel on his arm, here’s a look at Iowa’s next governor. Long before Democrat Rob Sand made political history, he was having a very different kind of moment in the spotlight, posing for Steven Meisel in the pages of Vogue Italia.

The year was 2004, and Sand was among the fresh faces who appeared in “Be My Baby,” an editorial in the February issue, photographed by Meisel and styled by Brana Wolf. Alongside Sand was a cast of rising fashion stars, including a young Caroline Trentini, with whom he posed in a series of moody, black-and-white portraits.

The concept centered on a romanticized vision of 1950s youth culture, abounding with the aforementioned rockabilly hair and lots of leather The original Italian introduction described the mood as a collision of bon ton—good manners and traditional propriety—and transgression.

Garren was responsible for the towering and teased hairstyles, while Dame Pat McGrath crafted smudgy, kohl-rimmed eyes. The cast wore Belstaff leather biker jackets, white Guess T-shirts and jeans, Helmut Lang tank tops and tailoring, Prada separates, and a Gucci crocodile belt. Womenswear included diaphanous Blumarine slips, Prada silk and tweed, and chiffon dresses by Rossella Tarabini for Anna Molinari.

“Was a fun day!” Sand wrote beneath an Instagram post by Meisel revisiting the shoot.

Sand’s modeling career was no mere college dalliance. While studying political science at Brown University, the six-foot-three Iowan spent time working in Milan and Paris, walking for Prada, cult Japanese label Number (N)ine, and Giuliano Fujiwara. He also appeared in Prada’s spring 2003 menswear show. In fact, his fashion commitments were once so serious that he missed the 2004 Iowa caucuses in his hometown of Decorah because he was working in Milan.

On November 3, Sand defeated Republican Zach Lahn to become the first Democrat elected governor of Iowa in 20 years, following two terms as state auditor. His newly resurfaced modeling portfolio has since become an unlikely internet sensation. Sand himself has joined in on the joke, sharing one of his old Vogue Italia photographs with the caption: “I’m disappointed to learn images of me modeling for Prada are circulating. Because this shot from Italian Vogue is much better.”

From Prada to public office, it’s quite the career trajectory. And with his campaign now selling “My Model Governor” merchandise, Rob Sand clearly knows how to harness a good fashion moment.

Kyle Larson Secures His Third NASCAR Cup Championship in Opening Stage of Championship Race

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

With his fourth-place finish in the first stage of the championship race at Homestead-Miami, Kyle Larson is a three-time NASCAR Cup Series Champion.

Chase Briscoe blew up with three laps remaining in the opening stage of the NASCAR Cup Series Season finale at Homestead-Miami, ending the stage early while Kyle Larson ran fourth.

With NASCAR’s new rule to end the stage if the caution is within 10 laps of the end, Larson took home the minimum seven points needed to clinch the Championship.

Larson was told he was one point shy over the radio, but he was 64 points above Christopher Bell and 76 points above Bell. He clinched the championship.

Kyle Larson joins the likes of Lee Petty, David Pearson, Cale Yarborough, Darrel Waltrip, Tony Stewart, and Joey Logano as a three-time NASCAR Cup Series Champion.

Through The Chase, Larson had an average of 49.5 points per race, easily rising to the top of the standings and then pacing the field from there.

Entering Homestead, it wasn’t given, but it was likely he could clinch the championship in stage one.

Spalletti: ‘Juventus wallow in doubt and lose confidence’

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Luciano Spalletti reiterated Juventus ‘can’t seem to be consistent’ and ‘lose confidence, so we wallow in doubt’ after a 2-2 draw with Fiorentina, but remained enigmatic on penalty appeals.

The Bianconeri had to fight back twice for a point in Florence this evening, as they went behind within four minutes to a Mateo Pellegrino header, then once Nick Woltemade had equalised with his first Serie A goal, Alieu Njie had restored the Viola advantage.

Gleison Bremer rose to meet a Douglas Luiz corner kick for the final 2-2 result, although both teams had late complaints against the referee.

“We tend to drown in a glass of water sometimes, and lose confidence in the way we play,” Spalletti told DAZN Italia.

“We conceded so early, but then reacted well, we just can’t seem to be consistent throughout a match, as a team or as individuals. We do really well for 10 minutes, then are lacking in something else the next, we aren’t consistent.”

The Njie goal raised eyebrows, because while Guglielmo Vicario had made the initial save, his teammates weren’t reactive enough to clear, allowing the Fiorentina player to bundle in the loose ball.

“In some moments we decided not to make decisions, we just stay there and accept the situation, without taking action or the initiative. Doing something takes away the doubt, but when you wallow in the doubt, you never manage to be in control of a situation,” confessed Spalletti.

It was a new trident attack this evening with Jeremie Boga and Randal Kolo Muani taking the wings, leaving Woltemade as the centre-forward.

“We made this choice because Fiorentina are aggressive, and seeing as we don’t build out too well from the back, at times we need to be more direct going for the centre-forward. It is not his greatest strength, holding up the ball, as he likes to combine with his teammates, but he’s the best at it in the squad.

“Kolo Muani is doing better, and with these fitness levels and rediscovered verve, you can put him anywhere and he’ll deliver a performance. He moves around a lot more and seems more engaged with the rest of the team.”

Radu Dragusin’s goal was disallowed for a push on Bremer, while Juve had penalty appeals rejected for a potential Joao Mario handling offence.

“I don’t know, do you have an alternative question?” replied Spalletti with a smile when shown the replay of the incident.

“I didn’t need a replay, I already saw it on the pitch.”

The result means that Juventus go into the break for international duty in seventh place, falling six adrift of Milan and the Champions League qualifying spots.

A tally of 18 points after 11 rounds is their worst start to a Serie A season since 15 points in 2021/22 under Max Allegri.
